  ```sh
  sudo -u zab-admin $PY -E -s -B -u $W/admin/zulip_provision.py --roster "$R" \
    --zuliprc /etc/zab-admin/secrets/provisioner.zuliprc --out-dir "$OUT" --repo-owner "$ORG" \
    --refserve-url "$URL" ${GIFTS:+--gifts-step "$GIFTS"} --no-rebind --regenerate-keys --only "$SLUG" --dry-run
  ```

  Then the same without `--dry-run`: its row reads `codex: key regenerated, claude: key regenerated`. **Never without `--only`:** it would give new keys to every active bot of the roster, and every running VM's bridges would stop answering until their next `push_config.py`. The row's VM gets its new keys at its own: an `!adopt-spare`'s, or the `!rollout` after a `!launch`.

  **Then the VM.** `!launch <slug>`, or `!adopt-spare <slug> zab-spare-N`, puts the row back in service from its `apply_start`, whatever its outcome: a launch that failed may have left a VM, which `all` must reach. `!status all` with every row retired says so. The refusal (`actions.BACK`) names the three steps, but the bot checks only the first, the roster: it can't see whether the bots were reactivated. An `!adopt-spare` of a row whose bots are still deactivated takes the spare, then fails at its `zulip_provision.py` step, whose new bots would need the deactivated ones' e-mail addresses, and the rest of it is a deployer's (ORGANIZERS §7.2). So reactivate the bots before you tell the organizers to send it. (A retired row stays in the roster, and is not one of `retired-slugs.txt`'s, which are the slugs removed from a roster, never reused.)

## 4. The read-only console

**Built** (#489, Oct 19): the collector (`zab.admin.collector`, with `costs` and `auditview`), the pages (`zab.admin.pages`), the console and its sign-in (`zab.admin.console`, `zab.admin.signin`); `deploy.sh` runs both units. What the build decided where this section left it open:
- **Most VMs are read by `!status`, not swept.** Every fleet runs idle stop (Q14), so the collector sweeps only running VMs whose last read shows idle stop off; a VM it has never read is not swept. Each `!status` the bots answer is kept for it per VM (`collector/reads/<ws>.json`), and a read that finds a VM down keeps its last values.
- **Plans** in the snapshot are the open plans read from the audit log, not `state.db`.
- **Prices** are a constant of the collector (`costs.PRICES`), not admin.toml's (§11.2): `config.py` is privileged (D32). Each VM's volumes are priced at the size EC2 shows (`DescribeVolumes`, read every 10 minutes since #553: §5's Read statement), from the first read that shows it; `launch.py`'s 200 GB where the role can't read it yet (a role without the volume statements: §5, "One file or several").
- **The token** is 64 hex digits (32 random bytes), which Zulip's Markdown leaves whole. It is consumed only by a sign-in that succeeds, and the bot, which issued it, writes `console_login` when its file is gone before its expiry.
- **Sign-out** deletes the cookie in that browser only; a copied cookie stays valid until it expires (12 h), unless its user leaves every allowlist, which every request checks. **To end every session at once,** replace the console's secret: `ssh -t $S 'sudo rm /var/lib/zab-console/secret && sudo systemctl restart zab-console.service'`; the console makes a new one at its start, and no cookie signed with the old one passes.
- **The link** travels in a Zulip DM, which missed-message e-mails and phone notifications can carry within its 5 minutes: ORGANIZERS §3.9 says to open it at once.
- **The collector and the console restart** when admin.toml changes (the collector exits for systemd; the console reads it again on the next request).

### 4.1 Data sources

The collector (`zab-admin-collector.service`, user `zab-admin`) writes `console/snapshot.json`, scrubbed (§2.7), with every workshop's fleet in its own section. Its sources:

| Source | How often | Gives |
|---|---|---|
| EC2, through the role, in each workshop's region: the VMs that `starter.fleet_vm` counts as the workshop's (§6) | every 60 s | state (`running`, `stopped`, `pending`, `stopping`), launch time (hence uptime), instance type, termination protection, volume size; the running hours it observes feed the cost estimate (§11.2) |
| `status.py --json` over SSH | every 10 min, for running VMs **without** idle stop only; and on every `!status` | zab version, Codex and Claude sign-ins, doctor failures, last activity, CPU load, memory, disk (`disk_percent`, and from #552 `disk`: the size, use and available space in bytes, from the same `df -Pk /` line), idle-stop timer and last verdict, and from #588 the sentinel (`sentinel`: `ok`, or the states of the bots' sentinels that warn, such as `down` or `stale, no mark`, from the doctor's `<bot>:sentinel` checks; null from a zab before it) |
| waker state (`/var/lib/zab-waker/status/waker.json`, which the waker writes every 30 s; from stage 4) | every 60 s | opted in or not, the state it saw last, the last wake attempt; and whether the workshop's waker is down (`waker down since …`, §1.8, #717) |
| audit log | on change | recent actions |

**Why not sweep idle-stop VMs.** Before #507, idle stop counted any SSH session, `ubuntu@notty` included, as a login, and restarted its grace period from the last one it saw (`workshop/admin/idle_stop.py`, `ssh_sessions`); it still does on a VM whose idle stop was set up earlier. It ticks once a minute (`OnUnitActiveSec=1min`), so it sees a short sweep only when a tick overlaps it. Regular sweeps would therefore delay idle stop at random, sometimes by a full timeout or more.

- For VMs with idle stop, the console shows the EC2 state, the waker data and the last sweep's values, with their age.
- **The reads are no longer logins (#507, P-a's first half).** `status.py` reads a VM as `zab-monitor`, whose sessions without a terminal idle stop doesn't count (`workshop/admin/idle_stop.py`, module doc). Its login shell is the read (`/usr/local/lib/zab/status_read_login`): whatever a session asks, and however it logged in, EC2 Instance Connect included, it runs `/usr/local/lib/zab/status_read.sh` as `agent`, which `/etc/sudoers.d/zab-monitor` allows and nothing more. The read's agent side is agent's own login shell (`.profile`) and `zab`, as `STATUS_CMD`'s always was; status.py's 180 s timeout ends the session. Its `authorized_keys`, root's, is rewritten from ubuntu's on every tick: each plain key, `restrict`ed (no terminal, no forwarding) to the same command. So a session of that user is the read, and no one but root could make it more. Only an Instance Connect key, which no `restrict` limits, may also forward ports and sockets, and use sftp where sshd serves it itself (`internal-sftp`), as it may for `ubuntu`. `push_config.py --idle-stop N` sets the user up, or resets one that exists; `0` removes it. Where the user can't log in, or its read fails on the VM, `status.py` reads as `ubuntu`, a login, as before: on a VM whose idle stop predates #507 until its next `!idle-stop` or `!rollout … idle-stop=N`, and where the copy leaves out the only key that reaches the VM (a line with options, a key in `authorized_keys2`).
- **Still to do (§8.3, P-a):** sweep every VM. The collector still leaves VMs with idle stop out, since one set up before #507 would count the sweep as a login.

### 4.2 What it shows

- **Who sees what.** An organizer sees only their own workshop. A global admin sees every workshop, with one summary row each, and can open any of them.
- **Cost** (§11.2), per workshop: month to date and projected to the month's end, both estimates, with running hours today; and per VM in its row.
- **Fleet totals:**
  - VMs by state, then the spares by state, apart (#715), as `!status`'s cost line counts them;
  - signed in: both, Codex only, Claude only, neither;
  - zab versions, with counts;
  - VMs under strain;
  - idle stop on; waker opted in;
  - doctor failures.
- **One row per VM:**
  - slug and state, with uptime;
  - zab version;
  - Codex and Claude sign-ins;
  - doctor failures (the first one, shortened);
  - the sentinel (#588): `ok`, the states of the bots' sentinels that warn, or `?`; for `not installed`, `down`, `no mark` and `stale`, the VM's page says that DM `!restart` is then unavailable and the bridge answers DM `!vm` itself;
  - last activity;
  - CPU, memory and disk bars, with the `!vm` colors and thresholds of #204 (green, yellow, orange, red; GB, not GiB); memory in binary GB, as `!vm` says it; the disk as `152 of 194 GB (78%)` (#552): the size and use of `df -Pk /`'s line, in binary GB as memory, `!vm` and `df -h`, rounded up as `df -h` rounds them (`pages.disk_gb`; `!vm` rounds its size to the nearest GB, so the two can differ by one, though the fleet's 200 GiB root, 193.6 GiB, reads 194 in both), with `df`'s own percentage: used ÷ (used + available), rounded up, which leaves the blocks reserved for root out of the denominator. A read kept from before #552 (the collector's state, a bot's `!status` feed) has no sizes, and shows the percentage alone;
  - idle stop (off, or on with N minutes and the last verdict);
  - waker (off, or on with the last wake); above the table, and on the list of workshops, `waker down since …` when the workshop's waker is down (§1.8, #717);
  - the data's age.
- **Recent actions:** the last 50 audit-view records.
- **Sorting.** It sorts by problem first: unreachable, doctor failures, strain.
- **Refresh.** `<meta http-equiv="refresh" content="60">`. There is no other JavaScript, except on the sign-in page.
- **No buttons that change anything.** Every row has a "request in Zulip" helper: a copyable command (`!start bob`) and a link to the workshop's organizers channel. Zulip has no URL that pre-fills the compose box (**VERIFY V8**), so the helper copies rather than pre-fills.

### 4.3 Serving

- **The server.** `zab-console` is a stdlib `ThreadingHTTPServer`, like `workshop/files/server.py`, so there is no new dependency. It listens on 127.0.0.1:8090 behind Caddy.
- **Routes.**
  - `GET` for `/` (the workshops the user may see), `/w/<ws>/`, `/w/<ws>/vm/<slug>` and `/w/<ws>/actions`;
  - `GET` and `POST` for `/login`;
  - `POST` for `/logout`;
  - `GET` for two static files, `/static/console.css` and `/static/login.js`, served from the release tree with fixed content types; they need no session;
  - everything else answers 404 or 405.
- **No inline code.** Every page links `/static/console.css`, and the sign-in page also loads `/static/login.js` (§4.4). Pages carry no inline `<script>` or `style` attribute, so the policy below needs no nonce or hash.
- **Headers.**
  - `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; form-action 'self'; base-uri 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` on every page. `form-action` doesn't fall back to `default-src`, so it is set explicitly. There is no `connect-src`, since the script makes no `fetch`.
  - Also `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` and `Cache-Control: no-store`. Not `no-referrer`: under it, a browser's form POST carries `Origin: null`, and the sign-in's POST must show the console's origin (same-origin POSTs, #496). The token travels in the link's fragment, which no `Referer` carries, and the pages load nothing from another origin, so `same-origin` sends nothing out (#496).
- **Caddy** is configured as for the files host: `admin off`, HSTS, no access log, the host of `console_url` (Q4) with an ACME certificate. It runs as `caddy`, which the nftables rule keeps away from IMDS (§1.2).
  - **Caddy holds a slow client, not the console:** it gives a request's headers and its body 10 s each (`servers { timeouts }`), and reads the whole body, at most 16 KB, before it passes the request on (`reverse_proxy { buffer_requests }`, Caddy 2.6's spelling; `request_buffers` is newer and 2.6.2 refuses it). The console's own deadline per request (10 s) and its cap of 32 at once stay, for local clients.

### 4.4 Sign-in

Recommended: **a one-time link through Zulip**, which is effectively signing in with Zulip.

1. An organizer sends `!console`, in their organizers channel or in a DM to any admin bot. The bot checks the allowlists.
2. The bot creates 32 random bytes and writes only their SHA-256, with the user ID and an expiry 5 minutes ahead, to `console/tokens/`, as a file it sets to 0640 before renaming it into place (§1.3), so the console can read it.
3. The bot DMs the organizer `https://<host>/login#<token>`, `<host>` being `console_url`'s. The token is in the **fragment**, so it never reaches a server log. Zulip's link previewer fetches only `/login`, which consumes nothing.
4. `/static/login.js` copies the fragment into a hidden field of the sign-in page's `POST /login` form, removes it from the address bar (`history.replaceState`), and submits the form. It makes no `fetch`.
5. The console checks the hash, the expiry, and the user ID against the allowlists (read from `admin.toml` on every request): a workshop's `organizers` see that workshop, `admins` see all. It deletes the token and sets the cookie `__Host-zab_console` (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, HMAC-signed, 12 h).
6. On every request the console checks the user ID against the current allowlists again, so removing an organizer ends their session at once, and a page of another workshop answers 404.

**Alternatives.**

- **GitHub OAuth** needs an OAuth app in the workshops' GitHub organization, `oauth2-proxy` or new code, and a mapping from GitHub logins to organizers: more parts for the same allowlist.
- **Tailscale only** would make every organizer join the deployer's tailnet. It remains possible as an extra lock, not as the only one; no setting offers it today.

## 5. Least privilege, per key

| Key | Where | Can | Can't |
|---|---|---|---|
| Instance role `zab-admin` (its managed policies `zab-admin-1` … `zab-admin-N`, `workshop/adminserver/README.md`) | the server, through IMDSv2, reachable only by root, `zab-admin` and `zab-starter`; the waker only through the starter (§1.2) | the EC2 actions below, only on resources in the server's fleet-wide scope (`zab-scope=<scope>`, and `[global] tags`) that name a workshop (`workshop=…`), in `[global] regions` | IAM, `PassRole`, other services, untagged resources, anything tagged `zab-admin` (the server itself), removing or adding the `workshop` or `zab-scope` tag |
| Fleet SSH key (ed25519) | `/var/lib/zab-admin/.ssh`, generated on the server | `ubuntu@` (sudo) on the server's workshops' VMs only: it is the EC2 key pair `zab-admin-<ws>` the server imports, one per workshop in its region, and launches with. On a VM with idle stop, also `zab-monitor@`, which runs only the status read (§4.1, #507), as every key of ubuntu's does; a key removed from ubuntu's is gone there at idle stop's next tick | anything else. It never leaves the server and is not backed up (§1.7). VMs launched before the server need its public key appended once by a deployer, over their break-glass key, with `join_server.py` (§8.4). |
| Admin bot zuliprcs (generic bots) | the global admin bot's in `secrets/global.zuliprc`; each workshop's in `secrets/bots/<ws>.zuliprc` | read and post in the bot's own channels and its DMs; download uploads there | create bots or invitations: Zulip refuses both to bots (`add_bot_backend` and `invite_users_backend` are `require_human_non_guest_user`) |
| Provisioner zuliprc | `secrets/provisioner.zuliprc`, passed as `--zuliprc` only to `zulip_provision.py`, `provision_repos.py`, `roster_import.py` (its live checks, and revoking its own invitations, sent before 0.2.10, to a corrected address at apply, §3.1) and `new_workshop.py` (`!new-workshop`, §2.9); the bot and the collector read it only to scrub its value (§2.7) | create bots and channels; revoke its own invitations; deactivate the bots it owns; read the GitHub profile field. It sends no invitations: a Moderator may not, where only administrators may invite, as in the org (#610), and the organizers invite in Zulip (#628, ORGANIZERS §1.5) | It is a human account's key, the most powerful Zulip key here: the dedicated `zab-provisioner` account, which a Zulip organization administrator creates, not their own; a Moderator (Q6, #397), so that it can bind rows by e-mail wherever a user's visibility includes moderators, without org settings or roles; and never subscribed to an admin bot's channel, because it owns the participant bots (§1.5, Q6). With this key, `!roster import`'s address check fails closed while any human's visibility is "admins" or "nobody" (§3.1). |
| Participant bot keys | `ws/out/<slug>/*.zuliprc` | what each bot can, which includes posting wherever its owner can (§1.5); pushed to its VM; copied for the waker when opted in (`waker/<ws>/<slug>/`, §1.3); used by the bot's wake monitor for `GET /messages` only, narrowed to the participant (§1.2, #359) | (also readable on the VM by its agent and its participant: SPEC §12.2) |
| GitHub token | `secrets/github-token`, read by the runner at each step that names it and passed as `GH_TOKEN` only to `provision_repos.py` (`!provision repos`, `!adopt-spare`), `roster_import.py`, which only reads a recorded login's access (§2.3, §3.1), and `final_sync.py` (`!final-sync`, `!retire`), which only reads whether each row's repository still exists (#714) | fine-grained, resource owner the workshop org, Administration and Contents read and write (`workshop/admin/README.md` §1b). Administration write **can delete any repository of the org**, earlier workshops' included. Its expiry must cover every kept month (Q7). Renewing it means replacing a secret on the running server, by hand: BOOTSTRAP §16 (#406). A GitHub App would replace it (P-c, not built). | the zab repository, other orgs |
| zab release deploy key (`secrets/release-key`, #569) | root only (0600), made on the server; only the upgrade's plan helper reads it, in a sandbox that hides the other secrets (§1.2) | read `admin.toml`'s `release_repo`, a deploy key of that repository: added read-only by its owner (BOOTSTRAP §13.4) | write; any other repository |
| Console session secret | `zab-console` only | sign its cookies | anything else |
| Rosters | `ws/<ws>/roster.csv`, 0600 | personal data, never committed, never posted except as plan diffs in the workshop's organizers channel and in the encrypted backups | |

**The instance role's policy** is rendered by `workshop/adminserver/render_policy.py --config admin.toml` from `[global]` alone: the scope, its extra tags, the regions and the instance types, and nothing account-specific (`arn:aws:ec2:<region>:*:…`). A deployer runs it on their own machine, from the signed release and their own copy of `admin.toml`, and the account owner pastes what it prints. The server never supplies the policy's text (§2.9). **VERIFY V4,** closed: the IAM policy simulator, then the e2e's positive and negative checks (A13; §9).

**One file or several.** A managed policy holds 6,144 characters, so the render writes `zab-admin-1.json` … `zab-admin-N.json`, one managed policy each, as `[global]` grows (`workshop/adminserver/README.md`). Every file carries every deny. For example, the MathAgents deployment's `[global]`, scope `mathagents`, us-east-2 and us-west-2 and one instance type, has rendered two files since the volume statements (#553):

```sh
python3 -I workshop/adminserver/render_policy.py --config ~/zab-admin/etc/admin.toml --out-dir ~/zab-admin/policies
# /home/<you>/zab-admin/policies/zab-admin-1.json: 5943 characters
# /home/<you>/zab-admin/policies/zab-admin-2.json: 1198 characters
python3 -I -c 'import json, sys; print([s["Sid"] for s in json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))["Statement"]][5:])' \
  ~/zab-admin/policies/zab-admin-2.json
# ['UsWest2Volume', 'LaunchNetwork']
```

A render with more files than the role holds is applied so that the role never holds less than either: the new files attached first, then the existing ones replaced, then the ones the render dropped detached (BOOTSTRAP §10, §6.5).

**One fleet-wide scope (#422).** Every resource of every workshop of the server carries `zab-scope=<scope>` (`[global] scope`), beside its own `workshop=<name>` and any extra scope tag of `[global] tags` (the test server's `zab-role=test`). Every condition below is on the scope, never on a workshop's name; in the table, "the scope" means `zab-scope` and the extra tags. Each region is set up once (below), so a workshop added in a region that is set up needs no policy change and no AWS step (§2.9). What the role creates must still name a workshop, any value (a `Null` condition): nothing it makes is in the scope but in no workshop, where no script would ever find it again.

**The trade-off, stated.** IAM no longer separates one workshop's VMs from another's: the role may stop, start or terminate any VM in the scope, of any workshop, in any of the regions. The server's code does: each bot acts only through its own workshop's argument lists, which name `workshop=<ws>` in every lookup, and its plans are approved only through its own bot (§2.1, §2.5); A15 tests it, and the starter, the collector and the plans' target sets check every scope tag of the VM's workshop (`starter.fleet_vm`, §1.2). The per-workshop conditions bought little: a compromised server already holds every workshop's keys, the fleet SSH key reaches every workshop's VMs, and the role served all of them anyway. What IAM still separates is the fleet from the rest of the account, which is the reason for the role's scope (§1.5): the account's other resources, the server, and anything not tagged into the scope, which the role can never tag there.

| Statement | Actions | Resource and condition |
|---|---|---|
| Read | `ec2:DescribeInstances`, `DescribeAddresses`, `DescribeSecurityGroups`, `DescribeImages`, `DescribeKeyPairs`; `DescribeVolumes` and `DescribeVolumesModifications` for `grow_disk.py` and the cost estimate's volume sizes (#553) | `*` (these describe calls can't be scoped), in `[global] regions` (`aws:RequestedRegion`). `DescribeInstanceAttribute` can be, and can return any instance's user data, so it is in the Lifecycle row (#329). |
| Console fingerprints | `ec2:GetConsoleOutput` | `instance/*` with `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-scope` = the scope **and** a `workshop` tag (`Null`: `false`) |
| Launch | `ec2:RunInstances` | `instance/*` with `aws:RequestTag/zab-scope` = the scope, a `workshop` request tag (`Null`) **and** `ec2:InstanceType` ∈ `[global] instance_types`; `volume/*` with the same tags; `image/*`, `security-group/*` and `key-pair/*` with `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-scope` = the scope (the region's, in no workshop; the key pair names its workshop anyway); `subnet/*` and `network-interface/*` unconditioned, the interface only as the launch creates it (the existing-interface deny below) |
| Tag at creation | `ec2:CreateTags` | `ec2:CreateAction` ∈ {`RunInstances`, `AllocateAddress`, `ImportKeyPair`}, with `aws:RequestTag/zab-scope` = the scope and a `workshop` request tag |
| Retag (`adopt_spare`) | `ec2:CreateTags` | `instance/*`, `volume/*` and `elastic-ip/*` with `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-scope` = the scope and a `workshop` tag, **and** `ForAllValues:StringEquals aws:TagKeys` ∈ {`Name`, `participant`}. IAM checks every key in the request, so the script sends only these two (§3.2). |
| Lifecycle | `ec2:StartInstances`, `StopInstances`, `TerminateInstances`, `ModifyInstanceAttribute`, `DescribeInstanceAttribute` | `instance/*` with `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-scope` = the scope and a `workshop` tag |
| Volume (`grow_disk.py`, #553) | `ec2:ModifyVolume` | `volume/*` with `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-scope` = the scope and a `workshop` tag: a fleet VM's volume, which `launch.py` tags as it tags the instance (Launch's `volume/*`), and `adopt_spare.py`'s retag leaves in the scope (it sends `Name` and `participant` alone) |
| Addresses | `ec2:AllocateAddress` (`aws:RequestTag`, as at launch), `AssociateAddress`, `DisassociateAddress`, `ReleaseAddress` | `elastic-ip/*` (and `instance/*` for associate) in the scope, with a `workshop` tag |
| Own key pair | `ec2:ImportKeyPair` | `key-pair/*` with `aws:RequestTag/zab-scope` = the scope and a `workshop` request tag |
| **Deny** | every `ec2:` action above except Read | any resource where `ec2:ResourceTag/zab-admin` exists (the server, its address and its security group, even if someone tags them into the scope by hand), and any request where `aws:RequestTag/zab-admin` exists, so that the server can't launch or tag a resource that its own role could then neither stop nor terminate |
| **Deny** (`DenyScopeRetag`) | `ec2:CreateTags` outside creation (`ec2:CreateAction` null) | any request with a scope key: `workshop`, `zab-scope`, the extra ones and `zab-admin`, in any case (IAM's tag condition keys ignore case). So nothing of the account is ever tagged into the scope, or moved between workshops, by the role; `DeleteTags` is denied outright (`DenyUntag`) |
| **Deny** (`DenyExistingInterface`) | `ec2:RunInstances` | `arn:aws:ec2:*:*:network-interface/eni-*`, an existing interface, in every region. EC2 checks the interface a launch creates as `network-interface/*` (it has no ID yet), which this never matches, and an existing one by its ID. Without it, Launch's unconditioned `network-interface/*` would let the server attach any detached interface of the account (`NetworkInterfaces=[{NetworkInterfaceId, DeviceIndex: 0}]`) to a VM it controls, taking another service's private address and security groups. `RunInstances` offers no `aws:ResourceTag` on an interface, so the deny can't be scoped by tag: it covers every existing interface (#356's V4 check; A13 probes it). |
| **Not granted** | `iam:*` (no `PassRole`, so no VM can get a role), `ec2:DeleteTags`, `CreateSecurityGroup` and `Authorize*`, `CreateImage` and `CopyImage`, everything outside EC2 | |

- **Setting up a region, once, by the account owner.** With the account owner's own profile, never the role, in the region (BOOTSTRAP §6.1 has the commands, and §6.5 the whole sequence for a region added after the bootstrap):
  - a security group `zab-fleet`, SSH only, as `workshop/aws/README.md` makes the fleet's (`launch.py` requires exactly TCP 22), tagged `zab-scope=<scope>` and `Name=zab-fleet`;
  - an AMI copy of the workshop image, with a dated name such as `zab-fleet-20261009`, tagged `zab-scope=<scope>` (the image and its snapshots). A newer image later is another dated copy, tagged the same; `!new-workshop` takes the newest (§2.9), and an existing workshop's `ami` changes only in its entry. Once no workshop needs an older copy, its tag is removed (BOOTSTRAP §6);
  - the region in `[global] regions`, then the policy rendered again and pasted (§2.9; BOOTSTRAP §6.5). That is the only time a policy changes, beside a new scope or instance type.

  Neither carries a `workshop` tag: they belong to every workshop of the region. Nothing else is made per workshop by hand: the server imports each workshop's key pair, tagged with its scope tags (§1.6). The server's own security group, Elastic IP and instance are tagged `zab-admin=<name>`, once, and never `zab-scope`.
- **History:** servers before v0.2.5 scoped the role by workshop name. v0.2.5 moved it to `zab-scope`, once the account owner had tagged the existing fleet into the scope (CHANGELOG 0.2.5).
- **The waker** (stage 4) does not hold the role. It asks `zab-admin-starter` to start a VM, and the starter calls `StartInstances` after its checks (§1.2). This supersedes the earlier plan that the waker use the role directly: "within what the server's sudo SSH already gives" holds for `zab-admin`, not for a process that takes any Zulip user's messages (#329). There is no cap on its starts (a cap's worst case is legitimate spend); the wake monitor (§1.2, #359) alerts the global admins about each start no message of the participant's explains, from the starter's log, which the waker can't write.
- **Remaining risk, stated.**
  - `ModifyInstanceAttribute` on a workshop instance can also change its user data, its type or its security group. That power is within what the server's sudo SSH already gives, on the same instances. **VERIFY V10** whether an `ec2:Attribute` condition can narrow it to the two attributes `protect.py` sets.
  - `ModifyVolume` on a fleet volume can also change its type, IOPS and throughput, and grow it far past what anyone asked for: a cost, never access. The role has no `AttachVolume`, no snapshot and no read of a volume's data. This adds no new kind of exposure: Launch's `volume/*` (`LaunchVolume`) already lets the role create a volume of any size, type and IOPS, with no `ec2:VolumeSize` or `ec2:VolumeType` condition either, and the large figures come from a type and its provisioned IOPS more than from a size. A type or IOPS downgrade could slow a participant's VM: availability, which the server's sudo SSH can take away anyway. The server's code sends a size alone, at most 1,000 GB (`grow_disk.MAX_GB`), and only through an approved plan. **VERIFY V18:** whether `ec2:VolumeSize` and `ec2:VolumeType` conditions can cap both statements in IAM.
  - A compromised server can destroy the fleets it manages, and only those: every workshop's at once, since IAM doesn't separate them (above). The defenses are termination protection on kept VMs (`protect.py`), the approval rules (two organizers, or a global admin, and the typed phrase, for a destructive plan; #558), the signed-release gate and the backups.
  - Two servers in one account need two scope values: IAM separates servers by `zab-scope`, not by workshop.

## 6. The portability seam

1. **Provider scripts with one CLI contract.** Everything provider-specific is a script in `workshop/<provider>/`, today `workshop/aws/`. The contract:
   - `launch.py --roster --only… [--spares N] [--protect] [--dry-run]` writes `host`, `ip`, `region` and `host_keys` back to the roster;
   - `power.py (--start|--stop) (--all [--spares] | --spares | --only…) [--wait] [--dry-run]`;
   - `protect.py [--off] --only… [--dry-run]`;
   - `teardown.py --only… [--dry-run] --yes`;
   - `adopt_spare.py --slug --spare [--dry-run]`;
   - each selects only instances that carry both the workshop tag and a `participant` tag, and no `zab-admin` tag.

   Each also takes provider-specific flags, which the bot passes from `[workshop.<provider>]` in `admin.toml`. Everything after the launch (Zulip, GitHub, SSH, the bridge) already works from the roster's `ip` and is provider-neutral.
2. **A `Provider` protocol: planned, not built.** The design is a protocol for the Python code that reads the fleet or starts VMs, the collector, the waker and the plans' target sets, with an `AwsProvider` that wraps `aws_common.Ec2` and the waker's `Regions`:

   ```python
   class Provider(Protocol):
       # Vm: slug, instance id, region, state, ip, launched_at, protected, spare.
       # Only instances with the workshop tag and a participant tag are VMs.
       def vms(self, ws: Workshop) -> list[Vm]: ...
       def start(self, vm: Vm) -> None: ...
       def stop(self, vm: Vm) -> None: ...
   ```

   **What exists today** is AWS code that shares one rule: `starter.fleet_vm` decides which instances are a workshop's VMs (every scope tag, a non-blank `participant` tag, no `zab-admin` tag, in the workshop's region). The starter (`DescribeInstances`, `StartInstances`), the collector's EC2 reads and `src/zab/admin/provider.py`, which finds the plans' target sets (`targets`, `tagged`: §2.4, §3.3) and only reads, each call EC2 through boto3 with it. Everything that changes the fleet stays in the scripts.
3. **No account IDs, no regions in code.** Region, AMI, security group, instance types, key pair, tag, GitHub org and file-server URL all come from the workshop's entry in `admin.toml` (§2.1). Policy ARNs use `*` for the account. The scope tags and the region have no default: every script of `workshop/aws/`, `roster_import`, `roster_sync` and `build_ami` requires them, and `status` its tags (#808). `us-west-2` remains only as the region of a roster row launched before `launch.py` recorded it (`status`, `power`, the bot), and as the default of `push_waker` and of the waker's own config (`config.get("region", …)` in `zab.waker`). These defaults stay for the manual path, but the bot always overrides them:
   - `INSTANCE_TYPE`;
   - `ORG` and `REPO_OWNER = "MathAgents"`;
   - `REFSERVE_URL`.

   The waker takes its workshop from its config's `workshop` key, which `push_waker.py --workshop` writes; the bot always passes it (§3, action 5). Both default to `nt-agents-2026`, the first deployment's, so a waker on a host of its own passes it every time (`workshop/waker/README.md`).

A later workshop on another cloud needs the scripts of item 1 in `workshop/<provider>/` and, once item 2 is built, one `Provider` class; until then the starter, the collector and the target sets are AWS code. The bot, the grammar, the approvals, the console and every Zulip and GitHub step stay as they are.

## 7. End-to-end test plan (on the #263 rig)

**Setup.** It runs on test infrastructure only, resources tagged `zab-role=test`.

- **Test admin server:**
  - a `t4g.small` in the test account, tagged `zab-role=test` and `zab-admin=e2e`, never `workshop=…`, with termination protection;
  - its instance profile `zab-test-admin` carries §5's policy, rendered for the rig's scope, `[global] scope = "admin-e2e"` with `tags = ["zab-role=test"]`, so `zab-scope=admin-e2e` and `zab-role=test` are in every condition (#422); its two workshops, `zab-admin-e2e` and `zab-admin-e2e2`, appear nowhere in it. The rig's region (us-east-1) is set up as §5 says, by the harness: the test AMI (#269), the workshops' security groups `zab-test-admin-e2e*` and the server's key pairs `zab-admin-zab-admin-e2e*` carry the scope, added to those made before it;
  - it is installed with `deploy.sh --bootstrap`, from the development machine, from the commit under test;
  - its config allows `rc` tags (`release = "rc"`);
  - its `allowed_signers` and `backup_recipients` hold a **test-only** key, kept on the development machine.
- **Zulip:**
  - **two test workshops,** each with its own bot and private channel, named to match `zab-dev*`, the only channels where coding agents post:
    - `zab-admin-e2e`: the bot `Workshop admin · e2e` in `#zab-dev-organizers`, with `zab-tester` and `zab-tester2` subscribed;
    - `zab-admin-e2e2`: the bot `Workshop admin · e2e2` in `#zab-dev-organizers-2`, with `zab-tester2` as its only organizer;
  - the global admin bot `Workshop admin · test` in `#zab-dev-admins`;
  - each test workshop's `alerts_channel`, `#zab-dev-alerts` and `#zab-dev-alerts-2`: private, with the test provisioner `zab-tester` subscribed, as `!new-workshop` subscribes `zab-provisioner` (§2.9);
  - `admins = deployers = [zab-tester]` and `destructive_approvals = 1`: there is only one test user who is a global admin. `zab-tester2` organizes `zab-admin-e2e2` only, so `zab-admin-e2e`'s bot doesn't recognize them (A1, A15). **A9 alone** runs on a variant (`harness/admin_toml.ending`): `destructive_approvals = 2`, `zab-tester2` a second organizer of `zab-admin-e2e`, and a 15-minute plan TTL; its first part installs it, its last puts the rig's back, and after a run that died between them the next run's setup does. So the two-approver rule is covered live by `zab-tester2`, an organizer, then `zab-tester`, a global admin (#356), with no third test user; and a global admin's approval alone by `zab-tester`, on the spare's teardown (#558);
  - provisioning runs with `zab-tester`'s key: a Member's until Oct 15, a Moderator's since, as `zab-provisioner`'s is (Q6, #397). That doubles as **VERIFY V5**: which provisioning steps that role may run, with `--hold`. The fake participants' bots are then owned by `zab-tester`, who is in `#zab-dev-organizers`, so they can post there, which A1 uses.
- **Fake participants:**
  - `zab-admin-e2e` has a two-row roster: `zab-test-a` (an e-mail that is never invited) and `zab-test-b` (`zab-tester2`'s address). `zab-admin-e2e2` has one row, `zab-test-c` (`zab-tester2`'s address), launched only by A18;
  - both provisioned with `hold`, but for A2's `!provision zulip zab-test-a`, which names only that row, whose address no Zulip user has; no invitation goes out (#628);
  - repositories `MathAgents/zab-test-a` and `MathAgents/zab-test-b`, test repositories, the only ones besides this one that agents push to;
  - VMs: `t3.medium` instances from the test AMI, tagged `zab-role=test`, `zab-scope=admin-e2e` and `workshop=zab-admin-e2e`.
- **Cost and time:** about 2 hours per full run. Three small instances run at once, over the project's limit of two test VMs at a time, which Q9 allows for these runs, at about $1 a run. Everything is stopped or terminated at the end, and the admin server is stopped between runs.

**Scenarios.** Each goes through approval to its result. The harness posts as `zab-tester` and `zab-tester2`, and as a fake participant's bot where stated, and reads the bot's replies.

**Stages 1 and 2, and the console:**

| # | Scenario | Pass |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Refusals.** `zab-tester` sends `!status all` (#466). `zab-tester2` sends `!status all`, `!stop zab-test-b` (its own VM), `!approve` on `zab-tester`'s plan, and the button reply. **Then `Codex · zab-test-a`'s key** posts `!stop all`, a fake plan, and a `zform` widget whose button replies `!approve P-<n> <real hash6>` for a real pending plan; `zab-tester` clicks it. **A command sent as code** (#546): `zab-tester` sends `` `!status all` `` in inline backticks, then, once it is answered, again. | `zab-tester`'s `!status all` carries the bot's 👀 within 5 s of being sent, and none of `zab-tester2`'s messages does; `zab-tester2`'s first message answered `🚫`, and the later ones, within the same 10 minutes, not answered; one `refused` record counts all four (written when the window closes, or when the bot stops); the plan still pending, nothing ran; the bot's first post answered `⚠` (§2.1: once per sender every 10 minutes), and one `foreign` record counts its posts; the click (`@**Codex · zab-test-a** !approve …`) is not a command, and the plan stays pending; the bot's own approval message for that plan (no phrase) has both buttons, Approve and Reject (#542); each `` `!status all` `` is answered with the hint (§2.2), word for word, the second as the first, and nothing else within 30 s: no 👀, and no `read` or `request` record from `zab-tester` |
| A2 | **Roster.** `zab-tester` uploads the two-row CSV with `!roster import`; then a CSV with a duplicate e-mail; then one that renames a launched row; after A5, one that changes `zab-test-b`'s e-mail (bound to `zab-tester2`), and one that changes `zab-test-a`'s e-mail (never invited: no `zulip_user_id`, and the old address matches no Zulip user); then `!provision zulip zab-test-a`, without `hold`; then one that changes it back | first: a plan with 2 added rows, approved, roster written, and an encrypted backup DM'd to the deployer (A14); the duplicate and the rename: validation errors, no plan; `zab-test-b`: refused as a rebind; `zab-test-a`: a plain change, approved and applied; `!provision zulip zab-test-a` (#628): its plan's dry run and its result both report the row `not on Zulip` under `invite`, with `-` under `zulip id`, and the line `zab-test-a: not on Zulip: invite <its new address> in Zulip (…)` under the table, and `zab-tester`'s `GET /invites` lists no invitation to that address; changed back, a plain change. The revocation of an invitation sent before 0.2.10 (#322) is tested offline only |
| A3 | **Binding.** Approve with a wrong `hash6`; approve after the TTL (test TTL 2 min); edit the roster between plan and approval; stop the bot, send an approval, start it | refused; `EXPIRED`; `STALE` with the diff; the approval sent while the bot was down is not processed |

A rerun within the hour of an alert for the same VM expects no new alert: its start is `held` by the VM's hourly limit, and that earlier alert must be on record. **Then** `!waker on zab-test-b`; stop the VM; `zab-tester2` DMs `Codex · zab-test-b`; then `!waker off zab-test-b`. **Then a waker down, and a restart after a busy topic** (#717): `!waker on zab-test-b`, the VM stopped; the waker stopped, as root (`systemctl stop zab-waker.service`, as an OOM-killed waker is to its VMs), until the bots say so; `!status zab-test-b`; `!waker on zab-test-b` again, a config change while it is down; then `zab-tester2` sends `Codex · zab-test-b` 12 asides (`//`, which the bridge leaves alone) of 9,000 characters, each edited 3 times, and a `!status` DM; the waker started at once; then `!waker off zab-test-b` | for `zab-test-a`: within 7 minutes the global admin bot alerts in `#zab-dev-admins`, naming the instance, "asked by root" and the failed check's reason (no `zulip_user_id`), and its `wake_check` is `failed`, with that alert's ID; for `zab-test-b`, the same, but `unmatched`. The waker (user `zab-waker`) starts it, and the bridge answers the DM (`Bridge running`; the `!login` hint may be missing on a VM just booted); that start's `wake_check` is `matched`, with the DM's ID, and no alert names it; after `off`, the waker config no longer lists it; as `zab-waker`, the provisioner key, the GitHub token and the fleet key are unreadable. Then the instance, its volume and its Elastic IP are tagged `participant=zab-test-d`, `Name=ws-zab-test-d`, and no instance is `zab-spare-1` any more; the row has the spare's address, `host` `ws-zab-test-d` and `zab-tester`'s `zulip_user_id`, and `spares.json` marks the spare adopted by it; `Claude · zab-test-d` and `Codex · zab-test-d` are active and `zab-tester`'s, with both zuliprcs in `out/`, and each is in `zab-test-d-sandbox`; the waker's config lists both bots with the instance and `allow_user_ids` `zab-tester`'s, beside the fleet's; `!status`'s table reads the rc in its `zab` column and `240 min` in `idle stop`, as status.py read them on the VM; on the VM, the idle-stop unit runs `--minutes 240` without `--spare`, and `/var/log/zab-idle-stop-spare.log` has a `stay up:` line naming `the first boot's volume warm-up is running` before its `power off: idle for 10 min`; and `Codex · zab-test-d` answers the DM `Bridge running`. Afterwards both instances are terminated and their addresses released, `!status` reads idle stop `off` on both VMs, the waker's config lists none of the four rows, and the roster holds `zab-test-a` and `zab-test-b` alone |
| A21 | **`!grow-disk`** (#553; `test_scenarios_4_grow_disk.py`), after A20 and before A9, on A4's two VMs, needing only them. **Refusals:** `!grow-disk zab-test-b 1001GB`; then a size 1 GB under the volume's. **A running VM:** `zab-test-b`, its file system's size read over SSH from the server (`df -Pk /` as `zab-admin`, the fleet key and the pinned host key), then `!grow-disk zab-test-b <size + 2>GB`, approved; then the same with 4 more at once; then `<size + 2>GB` again, the volume's own size now, approved (#563). **A stopped VM:** `zab-test-a`, its size read while it runs, then stopped, `!grow-disk zab-test-a <size + 2>GB` approved, and started; stopped again after, as A9 finds it. Needs the rig's role from this render (§5's `Volume` and `Read`) | the first refused by the grammar (`a size like 300GB, at most 1000GB`), the second at its dry run (`… GB already: ask for more (EBS only grows)`), the volume unchanged; the running VM's plan is a change with no phrase, its dry run names the volume, both sizes and `+2 GB: about $0.16 a month more`, and the disk's use; its result says `optimizing` or `completed`, then `root file system now: …`; EC2 shows the new size, and `df` about 2 GB more than before; the second request is refused for EBS's 6 hours; the third's plan says `already <size + 2> GB: its file system alone` and `would grow its file system over SSH (no cost)`, and its result has no `ModifyVolume`, `growpart … unchanged`, `… fills it already: nothing to resize` and `root file system now: …`, EC2's size and `df` as they were. The stopped VM's plan says its file system grows at its next boot, its result `the VM is stopped: …`, EC2 shows it stopped and grown, and after the start `df` shows about 2 GB more (VERIFY V17). A run within 6 hours of another on the same VMs skips the grows, naming the limit |
| A22 | **The roster from the workshop's channel** (#627; `test_scenarios_5_roster_sync.py`), on `Workshop admin · e2e2`, whose `channel` is `#zab-dev-participants-2`, with `zab-tester` (the test provisioner) and `zab-tester2` subscribed, which the setup makes (`ZulipRig`). Needs Q9's provisioner key. e2e2's roster is set aside, as root, and put back after. As `zab-tester2`, e2e2's organizer: `!roster sync`; again; `!roster remove <slug>`; `!roster sync`; `!roster restore <slug>`; `!roster rename <slug> zab-test-sync`; then `zab-tester2` unsubscribes itself and `!roster sync` again | the first sync adds one row, `zab-tester2`'s, by Zulip ID, its slug from its full name, and none for `zab-tester`, whose key reads the channel; the second has nothing to change: answered `☑`, with no plan, `nothing to change: every subscriber has a row, and every row is theirs` under it, and `!plan` reads it `unchanged` (#708); after the remove, the row is `excluded` (`!roster show` marks it) and the sync has nothing to change again, `☑`, its dry run listing `x <slug>: excluded`; the restore clears it; the rename keeps the Zulip ID; the last sync removes the row, nothing being provisioned for it. `zab-tester2` is subscribed again in the `finally` |
| A23 | **The rollout's defaults** (#704; `test_scenarios_3_defaults.py`), after A8 and before A20, on `zab-test-b` alone, a VM whose idle stop was never set: A4 launches it each run, and A6's rollout, with the waker off then, only probes it. Needs A5's bots. `!waker on zab-test-b`, then `!rollout <rc tag> zab-test-b`, with neither `lean-shared` nor `idle-stop=`, then `!status zab-test-b`. **Then, whatever came before:** `!idle-stop 0 zab-test-b` once the rollout was approved, then `!waker off zab-test-b` (ORGANIZERS §5.2: idle stop off, then the waker) | before the rollout, on the VM as `ubuntu`: no `/etc/zab/idle-stop.off`, no `/var/log/zab-idle-stop.log` and no idle-stop timer, and `/var/lib/cloud/data/instance-id` holds the VM's instance ID, read without sudo (VERIFY V19); the waker's config names that instance for both of the row's bots. The rollout's plan takes no phrase; its `push_config.py` carries `--lean-shared-default`, `--idle-stop-default 60` and `--has-waker zab-test-b=<the instance>`, and neither `--lean-shared` nor `--idle-stop`; its notes start `**Shared Mathlib (default):**` and `**Idle stop: 60 min (default)**`, and none starts `Not on`; its dry run names `lean-project + shared Mathlib`, `lean check`, `idle-stop probe` and `idle stop installed`. Approved, every step exits 0, the row's one idle-stop note is `idle stop: after 60 min` (never `… not this VM (unknown)`), and it has no `lean shared:` or `lean check:` failure. `!status` reads `60 min` in its `idle stop` column. On the VM, `/usr/local/bin/lean-project` is there, and `/home/agent/lean/workspace/.lake/packages` is a link into `/opt/lean/packages/` |
| A17 | **Month-end** (#570; `test_scenarios_8_the_end_of_month.py`), after A9 and before A18, so that at most three instances run, or alone, from no roster (the rig's setup leaves none), which its import makes; on rows of its own: `zab-test-r` (A9's `!retire` row, brought back by a deployer's fix of its `host` and `ip`, with `zab-tester2`'s `zulip_user_id` set by hand: it has no bots) and `zab-test-n` (no Zulip ID), both launched. `!protect on zab-test-r` and `!stop zab-test-r`; `!keep-until <in 15 min> zab-test-r zab-test-n`, then `!month-end schedule`, approved by a global admin alone; `!keep-until <a day later> zab-test-n` after the notices; once it has dropped out, its `zulip_user_id` set by hand to one no user of the realm has, and `!month-end schedule` again, approved in the same way (#883); the bots restarted while the run waits out the 5 minutes before `zab-test-r`'s teardown; at the end, `!retire zab-test-n unsynced` | the plan names `zab-test-n` as one no warning can reach; the notice DM reaches `zab-tester2`, and the schedule's topic says so, and that `zab-test-n` has no Zulip ID; `zab-test-n` drops out at the next look, without a new approval; with the second schedule, Zulip refuses `zab-test-n`'s notice (`Invalid user ID`, a 400) at every try, within 3 looks, and the row stops, its VM still there, the topic's line naming it with `!retire zab-test-n`, while `zab-test-r` keeps its notice, sent once (one notice DM to `zab-tester2` in all); at the end, a run: `zab-test-r` is started and synced, its protection lifted; the restart marks the run interrupted, and the next look resumes it at `teardown`, without a second start or sync; the teardown begins at least 300 s after the protection off ended (#378; the steps' `at` in `apply_end` and `month_end_steps`), and the instance is terminated; the row is `done` (`stopped` at its bots without Q9's provisioner key). With Q9's GitHub token, a failed `!provision repos no-invite zab-test-r` fails A17. Without the token, the run stops `zab-test-r` at its sync, its VM still running, and A17 skips after the clean-up, naming Q9 and what it couldn't check (the restart, the resume, the #378 bound), as does a run whose teardown's wait leaves less than a minute for the restart. A failure from the schedule's approval on first sends `!month-end cancel zab-test-r zab-test-n`, best effort, so that no run starts `zab-test-r` beside A18's VM |

**A9 in detail** (stage 3, PR 19; for the e2e suite, PR 16, #356). On `Workshop admin · e2e` in `#zab-dev-organizers`, as `zab-tester`, after A4–A7: `zab-test-a` and `zab-test-b` are running, provisioned with `hold`, rolled out, and `zab-test-a` is protected (A4). A9 runs on the two-approver variant of the test admin.toml (Setup, above): the spare's teardown (step 7) and step 6b's second `!retire` are approved by `zab-tester` alone, a global admin, with their phrase, which completes it (`approved (a global admin's approval)`, one approver in `apply_start`; #558); every other destructive plan is approved first by `zab-tester2`, an organizer, with its phrase, which is recorded as one of two and leaves the plan pending, then by `zab-tester`, which completes it (`approved (2 distinct approvers)`) and applies it; the 15-minute TTL lets step 4's teardown outlast the `!protect off`, whose `protect.py` reads EC2 back for up to 5 minutes (#378). "Approve" means the exact `!approve P-n <hash6> [phrase]` the approval message shows. After each apply, the step checks the result message (✅, every step exited 0), its `audit #… · chain …` footer, and the `apply_start` and `apply_end` records: `exit` 0, and `targets` listing the rows acted on.

**The throwaway row.** No verb reactivates a deactivated bot (only its owner or an organization administrator can, by hand: §3.3), and a new bot of the same name collides on its e-mail. So `!deactivate-bots` never touches `zab-test-a` or `zab-test-b`, whose bots every run reuses. Before A9 the harness adds a row for this run only, `zab-test-tmp-<runid>` (`<runid>` lower case, letters and digits), with an e-mail nobody uses, by `!roster import`; then `!provision zulip hold zab-test-tmp-<runid>` makes its two bots and its held sandbox. It has no VM. Then `!provision zulip zab-test-tmp-<runid>` releases the sandbox, which has never had a "Start here" (#709; `harness/start_here.py`): its plan passes `zab-admin-e2e`'s `refserve_url` and `gifts_step` (the rig's has one, with characters outside ASCII and a link) and shows the step in its dry run, and the posted "Start here" and the description are `zulip_provision`'s texts word for word, the file host in code and the bot names links to their DMs as Zulip renders them. The next run's setup empties the test roster, and the row was never invited, so nothing else holds it. A run that died before step 8 leaves the row's two bots active: the next A9 deactivates them first, by their owner `zab-tester` (bots named `Claude · zab-test-tmp-…` or `Codex · zab-test-tmp-…` only). Each run with Q9 answered leaves its row's sandbox channel, with its "Start here".

| Step | Send | Pass |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `!launch zab-test-b spares=1`, approve | `zab-spare-1` running, with its Elastic IP, in `spares.json`; `zab-test-b` unchanged (`launch.py` is idempotent) |
| 2 | `!uninstall all` | the approval without a phrase is refused: it needs the phrase `uninstall 2`; approve with it: both VMs `ok`, and `zab service status` on them shows no bridge. From here no agent works on either VM |
| 2b | `!export zab-test-a`, approve; before it, the harness makes a symlink and a hard link in `zab-test-a`'s `~/work` | the re-pack runs in the sandbox (#374): `journalctl -u 'zab-archive-check@*'` on the server shows one instance, run as a dynamic user, and no `tar` or `gzip` process ran as `zab-admin`; the stored `out/exports/export-zab-test-a.tar.gz` holds only files and directories, a copy for the hard link and `SYMLINKS.txt` naming the symlink, and the result reads `ok: 1 symlink(s) listed in SYMLINKS.txt; 1 hard link(s) copied`. Until Q9 gives the rows repositories, A19 runs the checker on the rig instead |
| 3 | `!final-sync zab-test-a`, approve | class change, no phrase; the lists carry `--strict`; `MathAgents/zab-test-a` has every branch of the VM (a rerun says `up to date`); its JSON report's `idle_hold` is `released` (#370); `apply_end` names `zab-test-a` |
| 4 | `!teardown zab-test-a`; then `!protect off zab-test-a`, approved and applied; then approve the teardown with `teardown 1` | the teardown is offered (the sync came after the uninstall): its plan lists `zab-test-a: uninstalled P-…, then synced P-…`, and its dry run `keep <id> ws-zab-test-a: termination protection is on`. The `!protect off` result says per VM whether EC2 shows it yet (#378). Then the teardown's approval makes it **STALE** before anything runs: the bot builds it again at apply, and the 5-minute wait after a `!protect` refuses it, naming the `!protect` plan and the time to request again. Nothing is terminated. (Before #378 it went STALE through its dry run, which the hash test of `normalize_teardown` covers offline: the test server's 2-minute TTL can't outlast the wait) |
| 5 | `!teardown zab-test-b` | refused at once ("no !final-sync after its !uninstall on record for zab-test-b: …"); a `refused` record; nothing changes in EC2 |
| 6 | `!teardown all unsynced`, at once after step 5; then again from the time it names | the first is refused by the wait after step 4's `!protect off` (#378): "P-… (!protect) changed termination protection here at …: no !teardown or !retire for 5 minutes after it. Request it again from HH:MM UTC", with a `refused` record, and nothing changes in EC2. The second, sent from that time: a plan whose approval message has no widget and shows the line to send as a code block (#542), and whose phrase is `teardown 3 unsynced`: `all` is the three rows, the throwaway row included, which has no VM and so is unsynced too (a VM with its tag would go). It lists `zab-test-a` synced, `zab-test-b: uninstalled P-…, not synced since: unsynced` and the throwaway row; its dry run lists both VMs to terminate and their two addresses to release. Approve with no phrase: refused; with `teardown 3`: refused (the phrase must say `unsynced`), from `zab-tester` and `zab-tester2` alike; the plan stays pending. With `teardown 3 unsynced`: approved and applied, both instances `shutting-down` or `terminated`, and no Elastic IP tagged `participant=zab-test-a` or `zab-test-b` left in the region |
| 6b | `!retire` (#559, §3.3), once step 6 has torn `zab-test-a` and `zab-test-b` down: three rows of its own, `!roster import`ed, none ever with bots: `zab-test-r` (the same slug every run), whose repository, `MathAgents/zab-test-r`, an owner made once (the notes below), and which `!provision repos no-invite zab-test-r` provisions each run (Q9's GitHub token), launched, then `!stop`ped; the run's own `zab-test-tmp-<runid>-r`, launched, never provisioned, `!protect`ed; and `zab-test-tmp-<runid>-n`, never launched. `!retire zab-test-r zab-test-tmp-<runid>-r export` at once, then from the time it names; approved by `zab-tester2`, then `zab-tester`. Then `!retire zab-test-tmp-<runid>-r zab-test-tmp-<runid>-n unsynced` (and `zab-test-r` too, when its happy path didn't run), approved by `zab-tester` alone. Then, with every row retired: `!stop all` and `!start all`, rejected or refused; `!status all`; the console's snapshot; `!launch zab-test-r`, or the run's own row's | the first request is refused by the wait after the `!protect` (#378, "no !teardown or !retire for 5 minutes"). The plan: phrase `retire 2`, **Rows that end** (2), the reminder to sign out, each row's VM as found (the one launched), its dry run `terminate` for `zab-test-r` and `keep` for the other. Its result: **the happy path for `zab-test-r`**, from its stopped VM: every step `ok`, its sync counting with no `unsynced`, its archive named, its VM terminated and its address released, `retired` yes; the other row `ok` up to its uninstall, `failed` at its sync (no repository: `--strict`), the rest `not run`, its line under the table, its VM still running and protected; `apply_end` names both rows and their seconds, recorded as the timings. The second plan: phrase `retire 2 unsynced`, its note that `unsynced` covers every row, `zab-test-tmp-<runid>-n: no VM found`; answered `(a global admin's approval)`; the sync `failed`, tolerated, then protection off, teardown and bots `ok`; the never-launched row's VM steps `skipped: no VM`, its waker, teardown and bots `ok`: `✅ … 2 of 2 rows retired; torn down unsynced: zab-test-tmp-<runid>-r (final-sync failed)`, the VM terminated, its address released. With every row retired, which the provisioner's key makes so, either way: `!stop all` and `!start all` never name them, nor pass `--all`; `!status all` names the three rows as retired, and reads none of them; the console leaves them out; the `!launch` is refused, since the roster keeps the row's `host` and `ip`, saying that a deployer clears them first. Without the key no row is retired, and the report says that these checks didn't run. Every other bot of the workshop is still active. Before each approval, the harness checks that no row has a bot key in `out/`, so the bots step finds them absent and never names `zab-test-a`'s or `zab-test-b`'s |
| 7 | `!teardown spares`, approved by `zab-tester` alone with `teardown 1` | the answer `✅ P-… approved (a global admin's approval)`, and `apply_start` names one approver (#558); `zab-spare-1` terminated and its address released; the rig's other instances (the #263 test VM, the admin server) untouched |
| 8 | `!deactivate-bots zab-test-tmp-<runid>`, approve with `deactivate-bots 1` | that row's two bots inactive (`GET /users/{id}`); `zab-test-a`'s, `zab-test-b`'s, `zab-test-c`'s and every other bot unchanged |
| 9 | the rebind of `zab-test-b`'s e-mail | the import is refused as a rebind, as in A2. (A rebind verb, not planned, #369, would have made it a rebind plan, needing `destructive_approvals` approvals, or a global admin's alone, and the phrase `rebind zab-test-b`.) |

Notes for the harness:
- A9 terminates the run's two VMs: run it last, after A8. The next run's A4 launches them again, and A5 reuses their bots, which A9 leaves active.
- **A23** (#704) runs between A8 and A20 (`test_scenarios_3_defaults.py`, by file name), on `zab-test-b` alone, so that its VM has never had idle stop: A20's `idle-stop=240`, then its `!idle-stop 0`, would leave a timer or the record of idle stop turned off on purpose, which the default leaves as they are. It leaves the VM as A8 does: running, with neither idle stop nor the waker, but with that record, which A20's explicit `idle-stop=240` removes.
- **A20** (#456) runs between A23 and A9 (`test_scenarios_3_late_joiner.py`, by file name). Its last part puts the fleet back as A9 expects it: `zab-test-a` stopped and `zab-test-b` running, neither with idle stop or the waker, and only their two rows in the roster. Without A9, the run's teardown terminates whatever A20 left. Since #715 its spare stops by itself before the late joiner gets it: that wait, its launch's first-boot warm-up and the load it leaves, adds up to 90 minutes (`SPARE_STOP_S`), during which only `zab-test-b` and the spare run.
- **A21** (#553) runs after A20 and before A9 (`test_scenarios_4_grow_disk.py`, by file name), and leaves the fleet as A20 does: `zab-test-a` stopped, `zab-test-b` running. Each run grows both volumes by 2 GB, which A9's teardown deletes with the VMs.
- **A22** (#627) runs between A21 and the console's A11 (`test_scenarios_5_roster_sync.py`, by file name), on e2e2 alone: it provisions nothing, and leaves e2e2's roster as it found it.
- **The order the harness runs** (`tests/e2e/admin/test_scenarios_8_the_end.py`): at most three instances run, the server's included (Setup), so the spare comes first: `zab-test-a` stopped, `zab-test-b` running (`launch.py`'s `scan_hosts.py` reads its key again), step 1, then step 7 at once (spares hold no participant's work); then `zab-test-a` started, steps 2 to 6, and 8. Step 6 sends `!protect off zab-test-a` again just before its first `!teardown all unsynced`, so that the guard's refusal is certain however long step 4 took. Step 9's `!rebind` is checked as not offered (`grammar.LATER`). After A9, A13's comparison of the role's fleet with A4's VMs has nothing to compare, and skips.
- **While Q9 is open:** the throwaway row is added, but its bots and step 8 need the provisioner's key and skip. Step 3 needs the GitHub token and A5's repositories: without a repository, `!final-sync zab-test-a` is refused by `--strict` at plan time, which the harness checks, then skips; the export runs, and the harness checks that it skipped both rows as not provisioned and made `out/exports` 0700, then skips (the archives' checks need a repository); and step 4 asks for `zab-test-a`'s teardown with `unsynced` (its sync doesn't count), which goes STALE as it does with Q9 answered. Step 6 tears both VMs down `unsynced` either way.
- Steps 3 to 6 need the runner to write `targets` in `apply_start` and `apply_end` (`fleet.ApplyEnd`): without them no row counts as synced, and step 4 is refused like step 5.
- Don't start, roll out or sync `zab-test-a` between steps 3 and 6: any plan that names it forgets its sync.
- **Step 6b's happy path needs a GitHub token that covers `MathAgents/zab-test-r`** (#559): `zab-test-r`'s sync can only succeed with a repository. A fine-grained token limited to some repositories can't create one, so an owner of the organization creates `MathAgents/zab-test-r` once, private and empty (no README), and adds it to the rig's token (Q9), whose permissions must include Administration and Contents, read and write. The token's value doesn't change, and the rig needs nothing new. If the organization requires approval of fine-grained tokens, that change of the token's repositories may wait for an owner's approval again: until then the token doesn't reach the repository. On this rig, an owner created it on Oct 23, and the token covers it. Each run's `!provision repos no-invite zab-test-r` then finds it (`exists`), pushes the template only while it is empty (the first run), and adds a deploy key for that run's VM, titled `zab ws-zab-test-r`. The keys of earlier runs stay on the repository: their VMs are terminated, so nothing holds their private halves, but each run adds one. Now and then an owner deletes all but the newest, with `gh`: `gh api --paginate repos/MathAgents/zab-test-r/keys --jq '.[] | [.id, .title, .created_at] | @tsv'` lists them, and `gh api -X DELETE repos/MathAgents/zab-test-r/keys/<id>` deletes one. (`zab-test` itself won't do: `provision_repos.py` names a row's repository after its slug, and a row `zab-test` would meet the participant suite's own bots, `Claude · zab-test` and `Codex · zab-test`, in its bots step.) Without the token, or if that provisioning fails, both rows stop at their sync in the first `!retire`, and the second takes both down `unsynced`; the report says why, and the unit tests (`tests/admin/test_admin_retire.py`) cover the happy path. The bots step reads Zulip with the provisioner's key: without it, it fails, and no row is retired (the report says so). Neither row ever has bots: a `!retire` deactivates its rows' bots, and every run reuses `zab-test-a`'s and `zab-test-b`'s, which no `!retire` names.
- Step 4's teardown is requested more than 5 minutes after A4's `!protect on` (A5 to A8 come between); run alone with `--scenarios A4,A9`, the harness waits that out first, or step 4 is refused (#378). Step 6's second request waits for the time its first refusal names; the test server's plan TTL doesn't matter there, since the refused request made no plan.

The harness writes the JSON report and the Markdown summary of #263, which is posted on the PR.

**Running it** (PR 16, #356): `ZAB_LIVE=admin-e2e uv run --with boto3 python tests/e2e/admin/run.py`, from a checkout of the commit under test, with `--dry-run` first. It sets up or reuses the rig, deploys a signed rc of that commit, runs the scenarios (`--scenarios go` by default: the go criteria of §8.2; `--scenarios A19` runs the export sandbox's check alone, with no VM) and always ends by terminating the fake participants and stopping the server, then checks that it did. `tests/e2e/admin/run.py` and `harness/rig.py` list its inputs; the keys, zuliprcs and the test `admin.toml` stay on the development machine under `~/.config/zab/admin-e2e/`.

## 8. How it was built

The design was built in stages, between Oct 13 and Nov 7, 2026, in priority order, so that a workshop could run entirely on Zulip and the web (#267). The stages are a record now: what isn't built is marked so, here and where each section describes it.

### 8.1 The stages

| Stage | What | PRs (§8.3) | E2E (§7) | Status at v0.2.19 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. core | several workshops, one admin bot each, one region each; the grammar; typed approvals with one approver, and phrases above half the fleet; the plan hash with target sets and reconcilers; the audit log; scrubbing; the foreign-sender guard; roster import (no rebind), provisioning, launch, protect, rollout, idle stop, start, stop, status; adopt-spare; later `!grow-disk` and `!roster sync` | 1–10 | A1–A7, A15, A21, A22 | built |
| 2. server | the server's units and policy, `deploy.sh` (bootstrap, deploy, wheels), backups | 11–13 | A10, A12–A14 | built |
| 3. ending | `final-sync`, `export`, `uninstall`, `teardown` (spares included), `deactivate-bots`, later `retire`; the destructive and rebind classes with two approvers | 18, 19 | A9, A19 | built; `!rebind` not planned (#369) |
| 4. waker | the waker on the admin server, `!waker`, `#<ws>-fleet-alerts`; a fleet launched before its server joins it (§8.4) | 21, 22 | A8, A18, A20, A23 | built |
| 5. console | the collector, the console, cost reporting per workshop | 14, 15 | A11 | built, but the sweep of every VM (P-a) |
| 6. upgrade | the in-band `!upgrade-server` on the global admin bot | 20 | A10b | built (#569; parts 1–6), from v0.2.19 |
| 7. new workshops | `!new-workshop` and its root helper (§2.9) | 23 | A16 | built |
| 8. month-end | the automated month-end per VM (§11.5) | 24 | A17 | built (#570) |

What a stage that isn't built leaves to people: a deployer's rebind by hand for `!rebind` (§2.5). Every stage keeps the manual path as its fallback: the scripts of `workshop/admin/README.md` and `workshop/aws/README.md`, from a deployer's laptop.

### 8.2 The go criteria

The admin e2e suite's default selection, `go` (§7, "Running it"), is A1–A7, A10 and A12–A15: what the first workshop run through the server needed to pass on the rig before its launch, with the server bootstrapped and a rehearsal from roster to rollout.

### 8.4 A fleet launched before its server

*History.* The first workshop's fleet, kept after Oct 9, 2026, was launched from a laptop before the admin server existed, and moved onto it in October 2026 (CHANGELOG 0.2.4 and 0.2.5: its tools and the cutover). That one-time cutover's runbook was here. What outlives it:

- **The tools,** for any fleet launched without the server's key. Each runs on a deployer's laptop, a dry run first, and its docstring gives its usage:
  - `workshop/admin/stage_server.py` stages the fleet's kept rows, their bots' keys and `known_hosts`, then runs again on the server, as `zab-admin`, into `ws/<ws>/`, which must not exist yet, refusing a slug another workshop has or had (§2.1):

    ```sh
    # on the laptop, in the checkout that ran the fleet: a new 0700 directory
    uv run python workshop/admin/stage_server.py --roster roster.csv --keep keep.txt --to ~/stage
    # copy ~/stage to the server, into a new 0700 directory $T of zab-admin's; then, on the server:
    sudo -u zab-admin /opt/zab-admin/current/.venv/bin/python -E -s \
      /opt/zab-admin/current/workshop/admin/stage_server.py --roster $T/roster.csv --out-dir $T/out \
      --to /var/lib/zab-admin/ws/<ws> --config /etc/zab-admin/admin.toml --workshop <ws> \
      --retired /var/lib/zab-admin/retired-slugs.txt
    ```
  - `workshop/admin/join_server.py` appends the server's fleet key to each VM over a break-glass key; `--remove` takes it off again (§1.7);
  - `workshop/admin/move_bots.py` gives bots made by hand to `zab-provisioner` (`--to provisioner`), or back to the caller (`--to me`), so that they can't post in the admin channels (§1.5, V15). **Its `--zuliprc` must be an organization administrator's key** (an organization owner's is one): since Nov 2, 2026 Zulip Cloud refuses a bot's owner change to any other key, the bot's own owner's included (`400 UNAUTHORIZED_PRINCIPAL`, "Must be an organization administrator"; #758). Its dry run says when the key isn't an administrator's, and then fails if a bot would move; `--apply` names the cause on each row Zulip refuses. On a self-hosted Zulip, whose policy may still let the bot's owner change it, that failing dry run doesn't mean `--apply` would be refused: the bot's owner may still run `--apply`.
- **The rules it followed, which every fleet follows:** one waker per bot, so a waker run on another host stops before the server's takes its bots (`workshop/waker/README.md`); a row bound to its participant's Zulip user ID before its `!waker on` (the wake monitor, §1.2); the waker before idle stop, so that no VM stops without a way to wake it (ORGANIZERS §3.5); and `zab-provisioner` subscribed to each workshop's `#<ws>-fleet-alerts` (§2.9).
- **If the server fails or may be compromised:** §1.7.
- **The role's policy changes** of v0.2.5 (the fleet-wide scope) and v0.2.9 (a second managed policy, for the volume statements): CHANGELOG 0.2.5 and 0.2.10, and §5 for the policy they made.
